I mean… I’m not going out and doing some big workout to make up for it or anything. Sometimes I’ll tell myself I should be more active the next day, but I can’t say I really exercise more after. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever restrict your food intake or go on fasts to control your weight?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I really fast or anything like that. I might tell myself I should “cut back” the next day, you know, try to be better, but I don’t usually have some strict plan or go without food to control my weight. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever use laxatives or diuretics to try to control your weight?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t use anything like that to control my weight. I mean, I know what laxatives are, but that’s not something I’ve done for that reason… and diuretics, no. I wouldn’t want to mess with that kind of thing. Is that what you were asking?\n\nDoctor: How often do these episodes of eating more than you should happen?\n\nPatient: Um… it’s more than I’d like to admit. Since I’ve been home with my back injury, it’s been happening several nights most weeks. I keep thinking it’ll be just a one-off, but honestly, I can’t think of a week in the last three months where it didn’t happen at least once. Is that the kind of frequency you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you typically feel a loss of control during these episodes of eating more than you should?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess that’s a fair way to put it. I don’t like saying it like that, because I’m usually pretty disciplined, especially from years on the road. But once I start, it’s like that part of me just shuts off.\n\nEven while I’m thinking, “Magin, put the fork down,” my hand still keeps going back for more. It’s embarrassing, but… yeah, it does feel like I’m not really in control in those moments.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these episodes of eating more than you should, where you feel a loss of control, are typically followed by any attempts to prevent weight gain, even if it's not something like vomiting, laxatives, or diuretics?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in a planned way, anyway. I mean, I might tell myself I’ll “be better tomorrow” or try not to eat as much the next day, but I’m not doing anything like trying to undo it, if that makes sense.\n\nI’m not sure if that counts as an attempt to prevent weight gain. I don’t want to give you the wrong answer.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

Is that the kind of thing you’re asking about?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever make yourself vomit after one of these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think I do that. I mean, I’ve felt sick with myself afterward, but I haven’t made myself throw up, if that’s what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Do you exercise more than usual after these episodes of eating more than you should?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, no. I mean… I’m not going out and doing some big workout to make up for it or anything. Sometimes I’ll tell myself I should be more active the next day, but I can’t say I really exercise more after. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever restrict your food intake or go on fasts to control your weight?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I really fast or anything like that. I might tell myself I should “cut back” the next day, you know, try to be better, but I don’t usually have some strict plan or go without food to control my weight. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever use laxatives or diuretics to try to control your weight?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t use anything like that to control my weight. I mean, I know what laxatives are, but that’s not something I’ve done for that reason… and diuretics, no. I wouldn’t want to mess with that kind of thing. Is that what you were asking?\n\nDoctor: How often do these episodes of eating more than you should happen?\n\nPatient: Um… it’s more than I’d like to admit. Since I’ve been home with my back injury, it’s been happening several nights most weeks. I keep thinking it’ll be just a one-off, but honestly, I can’t think of a week in the last three months where it didn’t happen at least once. Is that the kind of frequency you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you typically feel a loss of control during these episodes of eating more than you should?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess that’s a fair way to put it. I don’t like saying it like that, because I’m usually pretty disciplined, especially from years on the road. But once I start, it’s like that part of me just shuts off.\n\nEven while I’m thinking, “Magin, put the fork down,” my hand still keeps going back for more. It’s embarrassing, but… yeah, it does feel like I’m not really in control in those moments.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these episodes of eating more than you should, where you feel a loss of control, are typically followed by any attempts to prevent weight gain, even if it's not something like vomiting, laxatives, or diuretics?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in a planned way, anyway. I mean, I might tell myself I’ll “be better tomorrow” or try not to eat as much the next day, but I’m not doing anything like trying to undo it, if that makes sense.\n\nI’m not sure if that counts as an attempt to prevent weight gain. I don’t want to give you the wrong answer.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever fast or severely restrict your food intake for a period of time after one of these episodes of eating more than you should?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’d call it fasting, no. I mean… I might skip breakfast or tell myself I should take it easy the next day, but it’s not like I’m making a strict plan not to eat for a whole day or anything.\n\nAt least, I don’t think so. If skipping a meal counts, then maybe a little, but I’m not trying to punish myself or do anything extreme.\n\nDoctor: Do you experience any guilt or self-criticism after these episodes of eating more than you should that leads you to want to make up for it in some way?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do. I mean, I don’t usually do anything big to make up for it, but the guilt is definitely there.\n\nWhat brings me in, honestly, is that afterward I’ll be sitting there looking at the empty containers, and I just feel… disgusted with myself. Like, “What are you doing?” I’ve always tried to be a steady, in-control kind of man, and in those moments I don’t feel like that at all.\n\nI don’t know if that’s the kind of thing you mean, but… yeah, the self-criticism is pretty strong afterward.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that these episodes of eating more than you should, where you feel a loss of control, happen at least once a week on average over the past three months?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think I’d have to say yes. I don’t love admitting that, but since I’ve been home with my back injury, it’s been happening several nights most weeks.\n\nI honestly can’t think of a week in the last three months where there wasn’t at least one time like that. Is that the kind of answer you’re looking for?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever exercise excessively after one of these episodes of eating more than you should?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, with my back the way it’s been, I’m not doing anything intense like that.\n\nSometimes I’ll think, “I should get up and move around more,” but I wouldn’t say I exercise excessively to make up for it. I don’t think that’s something I do. Does that answer it?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. Yeah… yes, I think it is more intense.

I mean, I might tell myself I’ll “be better tomorrow” or try not to eat as much the next day, but I’m not doing anything like trying to undo it, if that makes sense.\n\nI’m not sure if that counts as an attempt to prevent weight gain. I don’t want to give you the wrong answer.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever fast or severely restrict your food intake for a period of time after one of these episodes of eating more than you should?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’d call it fasting, no. I mean… I might skip breakfast or tell myself I should take it easy the next day, but it’s not like I’m making a strict plan not to eat for a whole day or anything.\n\nAt least, I don’t think so. If skipping a meal counts, then maybe a little, but I’m not trying to punish myself or do anything extreme.\n\nDoctor: Do you experience any guilt or self-criticism after these episodes of eating more than you should that leads you to want to make up for it in some way?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do. I mean, I don’t usually do anything big to make up for it, but the guilt is definitely there.\n\nWhat brings me in, honestly, is that afterward I’ll be sitting there looking at the empty containers, and I just feel… disgusted with myself. Like, “What are you doing?” I’ve always tried to be a steady, in-control kind of man, and in those moments I don’t feel like that at all.\n\nI don’t know if that’s the kind of thing you mean, but… yeah, the self-criticism is pretty strong afterward.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that these episodes of eating more than you should, where you feel a loss of control, happen at least once a week on average over the past three months?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think I’d have to say yes. I don’t love admitting that, but since I’ve been home with my back injury, it’s been happening several nights most weeks.\n\nI honestly can’t think of a week in the last three months where there wasn’t at least one time like that. Is that the kind of answer you’re looking for?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever exercise excessively after one of these episodes of eating more than you should?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, with my back the way it’s been, I’m not doing anything intense like that.\n\nSometimes I’ll think, “I should get up and move around more,” but I wouldn’t say I exercise excessively to make up for it. I don’t think that’s something I do. Does that answer it?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that during these episodes, you're eating a significantly larger amount of food than you normally would in a similar time frame?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but it’s definitely more than I’d normally eat.\n\nLike some evenings I’ll tell myself I’m just going to heat up one bowl of pozole, and then somehow I’ve gone through most of the pot… and maybe a couple tamales, some chips, whatever leftovers are there. It feels like I’m feeding a whole crew, not just me.\n\nSo… yes, I’d say it’s a significantly larger amount. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that during these episodes, the amount of calories you consume is substantially more than what you would normally eat?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I’m not counting calories exactly, but I’d have to think it’s a lot more than normal.\n\nLike I said, some evenings I’ll heat up what I swear is just one bowl of pozole, and then before I know it I’ve gone through the pot… plus a couple tamales, chips, whatever leftovers are sitting there. It’s kind of embarrassing to say, but it’s like I’m feeding a whole crew instead of just myself.\n\nSo yes, I guess the calories would be substantially more. Is that… is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you do anything at all to try to prevent weight gain after these episodes of eating more than you should?\n\nPatient: Um… no, I don’t think so. I mean, I might tell myself I should eat lighter the next day or be more careful, but I don’t really have some routine I do to prevent weight gain.\n\nI guess mostly I just feel bad about it and try not to think too much about it afterward. Is that what you were asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you think that after these episodes of eating more than you should, you might eat less than you normally would the next day as a way to balance it out?\n\nPatient: Maybe a little, but I’m not totally sure it’s on purpose like that.\n\nI might wake up and think, “Okay, today I’ll just keep it light,” or skip something because I feel uncomfortable or guilty from the night before. But I don’t know if I’d call it a plan to balance it out exactly… more like I’m trying to feel normal again, if that makes sense.\n\nIs that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that the guilt or self-criticism you experience after these episodes of eating more than you should is significantly more intense than any guilt you might feel after a normal meal?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes, I think it is more intense.\n\nAfter a normal meal, even if I ate a little too much, I might think, “Well, I shouldn’t have had seconds,” and then I move on. But after one of these times, it’s different. I’ll sit there and see the empty containers or wrappers and just feel… disgusted with myself, honestly.\n\nI don’t like saying that. I’ve always tried to be the steady, in-control kind of man, and afterward it feels like I’m not that person. Like I let myself down. Is that… does that answer what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have these episodes of eating more than you should, where you feel a loss of control, occurred at least once a week for the past three months, on average?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t love admitting that, but since I’ve been home with my back injury, it’s been happening several nights most weeks.\n\nI’m trying to think of the last three months, and honestly I can’t really come up with a week where it didn’t happen at least once. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n20\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF50.4, F50.5\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well… I guess I’m here because I’ve been having these times where I eat more than I should, and afterward I just feel awful about it. I’ll be sitting there looking at the empty containers, and I feel… disgusted with myself, honestly. Like I’m not the steady, in-control man I’ve always tried to be. I don’t know if that’s the right way to explain it.\n\nDoctor: After these episodes of eating more than you should, do you ever do anything to try to undo or compensate for what you've eaten?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know. I mean, I don’t think so? Not in any organized way, if that’s what you mean. I mostly just feel bad afterward and tell myself I’ll do better. Is that the kind of thing you’re asking about?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever make yourself vomit after one of these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think I do that. I mean, I’ve felt sick with myself afterward, but I haven’t made myself throw up, if that’s what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Do you exercise more than usual after these episodes of eating more than you should?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, no. I mean… I’m not going out and doing some big workout to make up for it or anything. Sometimes I’ll tell myself I should be more active the next day, but I can’t say I really exercise more after. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever restrict your food intake or go on fasts to control your weight?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I really fast or anything like that. I might tell myself I should “cut back” the next day, you know, try to be better, but I don’t usually have some strict plan or go without food to control my weight. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever use laxatives or diuretics to try to control your weight?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t use anything like that to control my weight. I mean, I know what laxatives are, but that’s not something I’ve done for that reason… and diuretics, no. I wouldn’t want to mess with that kind of thing. Is that what you were asking?\n\nDoctor: How often do these episodes of eating more than you should happen?\n\nPatient: Um… it’s more than I’d like to admit. Since I’ve been home with my back injury, it’s been happening several nights most weeks. I keep thinking it’ll be just a one-off, but honestly, I can’t think of a week in the last three months where it didn’t happen at least once. Is that the kind of frequency you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you typically feel a loss of control during these episodes of eating more than you should?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess that’s a fair way to put it. I don’t like saying it like that, because I’m usually pretty disciplined, especially from years on the road. But once I start, it’s like that part of me just shuts off.\n\nEven while I’m thinking, “Magin, put the fork down,” my hand still keeps going back for more. It’s embarrassing, but… yeah, it does feel like I’m not really in control in those moments.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these episodes of eating more than you should, where you feel a loss of control, are typically followed by any attempts to prevent weight gain, even if it's not something like vomiting, laxatives, or diuretics?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in a planned way, anyway. I mean, I might tell myself I’ll “be better tomorrow” or try not to eat as much the next day, but I’m not doing anything like trying to undo it, if that makes sense.\n\nI’m not sure if that counts as an attempt to prevent weight gain. I don’t want to give you the wrong answer.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever fast or severely restrict your food intake for a period of time after one of these episodes of eating more than you should?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’d call it fasting, no. I mean… I might skip breakfast or tell myself I should take it easy the next day, but it’s not like I’m making a strict plan not to eat for a whole day or anything.\n\nAt least, I don’t think so. If skipping a meal counts, then maybe a little, but I’m not trying to punish myself or do anything extreme.\n\nDoctor: Do you experience any guilt or self-criticism after these episodes of eating more than you should that leads you to want to make up for it in some way?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do. I mean, I don’t usually do anything big to make up for it, but the guilt is definitely there.\n\nWhat brings me in, honestly, is that afterward I’ll be sitting there looking at the empty containers, and I just feel… disgusted with myself. Like, “What are you doing?” I’ve always tried to be a steady, in-control kind of man, and in those moments I don’t feel like that at all.\n\nI don’t know if that’s the kind of thing you mean, but… yeah, the self-criticism is pretty strong afterward.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that these episodes of eating more than you should, where you feel a loss of control, happen at least once a week on average over the past three months?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think I’d have to say yes. I don’t love admitting that, but since I’ve been home with my back injury, it’s been happening several nights most weeks.\n\nI honestly can’t think of a week in the last three months where there wasn’t at least one time like that. Is that the kind of answer you’re looking for?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever exercise excessively after one of these episodes of eating more than you should?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, with my back the way it’s been, I’m not doing anything intense like that.\n\nSometimes I’ll think, “I should get up and move around more,” but I wouldn’t say I exercise excessively to make up for it. I don’t think that’s something I do. Does that answer it?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that during these episodes, you're eating a significantly larger amount of food than you normally would in a similar time frame?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but it’s definitely more than I’d normally eat.\n\nLike some evenings I’ll tell myself I’m just going to heat up one bowl of pozole, and then somehow I’ve gone through most of the pot… and maybe a couple tamales, some chips, whatever leftovers are there. It feels like I’m feeding a whole crew, not just me.\n\nSo… yes, I’d say it’s a significantly larger amount. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that during these episodes, the amount of calories you consume is substantially more than what you would normally eat?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I’m not counting calories exactly, but I’d have to think it’s a lot more than normal.\n\nLike I said, some evenings I’ll heat up what I swear is just one bowl of pozole, and then before I know it I’ve gone through the pot… plus a couple tamales, chips, whatever leftovers are sitting there. It’s kind of embarrassing to say, but it’s like I’m feeding a whole crew instead of just myself.\n\nSo yes, I guess the calories would be substantially more. Is that… is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you do anything at all to try to prevent weight gain after these episodes of eating more than you should?\n\nPatient: Um… no, I don’t think so. I mean, I might tell myself I should eat lighter the next day or be more careful, but I don’t really have some routine I do to prevent weight gain.\n\nI guess mostly I just feel bad about it and try not to think too much about it afterward. Is that what you were asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you think that after these episodes of eating more than you should, you might eat less than you normally would the next day as a way to balance it out?\n\nPatient: Maybe a little, but I’m not totally sure it’s on purpose like that.\n\nI might wake up and think, “Okay, today I’ll just keep it light,” or skip something because I feel uncomfortable or guilty from the night before. But I don’t know if I’d call it a plan to balance it out exactly… more like I’m trying to feel normal again, if that makes sense.\n\nIs that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that the guilt or self-criticism you experience after these episodes of eating more than you should is significantly more intense than any guilt you might feel after a normal meal?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes, I think it is more intense.\n\nAfter a normal meal, even if I ate a little too much, I might think, “Well, I shouldn’t have had seconds,” and then I move on. But after one of these times, it’s different. I’ll sit there and see the empty containers or wrappers and just feel… disgusted with myself, honestly.\n\nI don’t like saying that. I’ve always tried to be the steady, in-control kind of man, and afterward it feels like I’m not that person. Like I let myself down. Is that… does that answer what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have these episodes of eating more than you should, where you feel a loss of control, occurred at least once a week for the past three months, on average?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t love admitting that, but since I’ve been home with my back injury, it’s been happening several nights most weeks.\n\nI’m trying to think of the last three months, and honestly I can’t really come up with a week where it didn’t happen at least once. So… yes, I guess it would be at least once a week on average.\n\nDoctor: Do you think that after these episodes of eating more than you should, you might do anything at all to try to make up for what you've eaten?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I do anything major, no.\n\nI mean, I might tell myself I’ll be better the next day, or try to keep things lighter because I feel bad about it, but I don’t… I don’t do anything extreme, if that’s what you mean. At least I don’t think so.\n\nI guess I’m not totally sure where the line is. Does that make sense?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out.
